This is reminiscent of "continuous or discrete Radon transform" or "sumset". We want for each sum k, total weight of pairs (i,j) with i+j=k small. This is exactly minimizing maximum row sum of a symmetric matrix M_{i,j}=p_i p_j when restricted to antidiagonals i+j=k. We choose diagonal weights p_i to make all anti-diagonal sums bounded. Optimizer details:
- Input p (prob vector). Compute C using FFT. Score = 2n*Cmax.
- Maintain best, best_score.
- For phase, for it in range until time:
   - beta = beta_start + (beta_end-beta_start)*it/its or exponential.

   - P = rfft(p,N); corr = irfft(rfft(Z,N)*conj(P),N)[:n]. But we need P for C; if C stored from P? We can recompute P each iteration after p update. In loop:
      P = rfft(p,N)
      C = irfft(P*P,N)[:2n-1] (round small negative to 0? C nonnegative but numerical tiny negative; clip)
      ...
      grad = p + corr
      v = mom*v + (1-mom)*grad
      for trial_lr in [lr*0.5, lr, lr*2]? p_try = p - trial_lr*v; p_try=clip norm; score_try=2n*conv(p_try).max. Choose best trial (including no update). This is costly (extra FFT for trials). Maybe only every 100 iters do line search. Otherwise use p_new=clip norm and accept if smoothed L decreases? We can compute L_beta from C: (1/β)*log(∑exp(βC)).       Simpler: use a fixed lr with momentum, no acceptance; projected gradient descent on nonconvex may diverge if lr too large. Use small lr (e.g., 0.1) and clip norm. Since p changes slowly, likely stable.
   - To escape plateaus, every K iterations, if no score improvement for K, add noise: p = (1-η)*p + η*r, r random Dirichlet or Gaussian, normalize. Maybe also try reversing p.
   - Track best actual score.

Another way: The LP constraints A g ≤ M. We can solve the dual via a simple iterative proportional fitting (IPF) because constraints are sliding weighted sums. Dual: min ∑ y s.t. B y ≥1, y≥0. This is a covering LP. There is an algorithm: initialize y uniform; compute violation v_i=(B y)_i; update y_k *= η^{?} for constraints not met? For covering, multiplicative weights: y_k ← y_k * exp(η (B y)_k? no). We can approximate; maybe easier than full LP. But using approximate LP update may still help.

Let's derive MW for dual covering: Primal max 1^T g s.t. A g ≤ M, g≥0. We can use standard MW for packing (max c^T x s.t. A x ≤ b). Algorithm (Lancien):
Set x=ε c/n? y=1/m. For τ=1..T:
  x_i ← x_i * exp(η [1 - (A^T y)_i/c_i])? 
  y_k ← y_k * exp(η [(A x)_k/b_k -1])?
After T iterations, x/(1+ηT) approximates primal. Maybe we can implement and tune. But too risky.

Let's design `gd_optimize` robustly:
```
def gd_optimize(p, budget_s, name):
    n=len(p); N=next_pow2(3*n-1)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(p)
    p=p.copy(); v=np.zeros(n)
    beta=2.0*n
    lr=0.5
    mom=0.85
    stall=0
    kicks=0
    phases = [ (2*n, 50*n, 1.0, 0.2, 0.8, ...), ...]
    for phase:
      for it until phase end:
        P=rfft(p,N); C=irfft(P*P,N)[:2n-1]; C=np.maximum(C,0)
        z=beta*C; z-=z.max(); Z=exp(z); if sum0 onehot else /sum
        ZF=rfft(Z,N); corr=irfft(ZF*conj(P),N)[:n]
        grad=p+corr
        v=mom*v+(1-mom)*grad
        # optional normalize gradient by its mean to make lr scale invariant
        gmean = v.mean()
        # Try a few lrs: base, base*0.5, base*2? choose best score among maybe 2
        # To save, compute score of one step; but use C_new with FFT
        for lr_try in [lr, lr*0.5]:
            p_try = np.clip(p - lr_try*v,0,None)
            if p_try.sum()<=0: continue
            p_try /= p_try.sum()
            s_try=score_fft(p_try)
            choose best lr_try
        if best tried fails (score worse than current by >), reduce lr
        update v etc
        if score < best: stall=0 else stall++
        if stall>200: kick: p = normalize((1-e)*p + e*rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.1)) or p + noise; v=zeros; lr=base; stall=0
```
But scoring each p_try using FFT each iteration (1-2 extra conv) may be expensive but acceptable for moderate iterations. We can reduce n to 2048 for many runs and 4096 for few.

Can we reduce N for corr: If p is nonnegative, C, Z real. Use rfft. Score_fft just P=rfft(p,Ns), C=irfft(P*P,Ns)[:2n-1]. Ns=next_pow2(2n-1) maybe smaller than Ncorr. For line search, use Ns.

Need manage total time. The final code can run 1750s. It should print progress.

Potential pitfall: `np.fft.irfft` returns tiny negative due to floating; conv of nonnegative should be >=0. Clip C to 0 before max. For P*P, imaginary numerical.

Now, can we make a stronger initial shape that likely below target? Let's attempt to numerically reason about a candidate: p_i ∝ 1/√(i+1) * (1 - c * i/(n)) with c small positive (taper to right). This is getting complex. We can simply in the program scan a variety of bump mixes numerically (score FFT) and use best as start. That's practical. For each n, generate candidate sequences: base inv sqrt, add normalized bump with weights and parameters, evaluate score. Since scoring FFT cheap, we can scan many.

Bumps candidates:
- h_{a,b}(i) = (i/n)^a * ((n-1-i)/(n-1))^b, with a,b in {0.25,0.5,1,2,4}.
- sin^2, sin^4, triangular, etc.
Mix p = normalize((1-w)*base + w*h_norm). Also consider base reversed? Reverse mix.
Also consider base with a "notch": base * (1 - w * h) normalized to reduce middle while keeping nonnegative; but adding bump is equivalent with negative? If base*h product nonnegative.

Maybe adding a bump can lower max. We'll test in code mentally impossible, but likely some mix improves.

We can also scan a two-parameter family: p_i ∝ x^{-1/2} + A x^{p}(1-x)^{q}. Normalize; A can be positive or negative (if nonnegative). Since positive bump might increase center depending; negative bump (subtract mass in middle) could reduce center but edge unaffected? We can search A in [-0.9,0] maybe, ensuring nonnegative. For example p_i ∝ x^{-1/2} (1 - w x^c(1-x)^c) (notch). This subtracts from middle, reduces some lags; mass renormalized may increase edges, but maybe beneficial. We can scan w.

Given the code can evaluate, we can do a coarse random search over shapes and use best.

Another simple family: p_i ∝ x^{-1/2} * exp(-λ x) (exponential taper). Normalize. This adds negative power terms, edge increases? Exponential taper reduces mass in middle/right; normalization increases base, edge maybe increases. But may reduce off-center peaks. We can scan λ.

The program can generate a list of 100 candidate initial sequences for a chosen n, score them via FFT, pick top few, then run GD from each. That should find a good local optimum.

Let's set final strategy:
- Use n = 4096 (or choose among 2048,4096 based on time).
- Stage 1: Candidate generation and scoring (time ~60s). Shapes:
   * inv_sqrt with index shift 0.5
   * rev(inv_sqrt)
   * power x^{-α} for α in [0.1,0.5]? But α≤0.5; normalize; maybe α=0.5 best.
   * inv_sqrt * exp(-λ x) for λ [0,0.5,1,2]
   * inv_sqrt * (1 - w * bump) for bumps and w [0.2,0.5,0.8]
   * mixtures (1-w)*inv + w*bump_norm for bumps and w [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5]
   * mixtures with a "right-shifted inv" maybe: base_i = 1/sqrt(i+1) and shifted base_j =1/sqrt(i+M+1) differences? E.g., p_i = v_i - q v_{i+M} positive.
   * parent normalized if score good.
   Scoring all using `score_fft`. Keep top 10 unique (by maybe first few bits).
- Stage 2: For each top candidate, run `gd_optimize` with a time slice (e.g., total GD time split evenly among top 5, at least 30s each). Use best so far as global.
- Stage 3: Random local search from best: block perturbations / additive noise for remaining time, accept if score improves.
- Final: maybe run final polish from global best using very high beta and small lr for a few hundred/s iterations.
- Return best sequence clipped [0,1000] (values small) as list floats.

Need make code concise but complete. Top-level helper functions no closures/lambdas. No imports of evaluate_sequence. Use time.monotonic.

Let's tune `gd_optimize` algorithm.

Pseudo:
```
def fft_score(p):
    n=len(p); N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)[:2*n-1]
    return 2.0*n*C.max()
```
For gradient:
```
def gd_step(p, beta, N, mom, v, lr):
    P=...
    C=...
    z=beta*C; zm=z.max(); Z=np.exp(z-zm); s=Z.sum(); if s==0...
    ZF=...
    corr=...
    grad=p+corr
    v=mom*v+(1-mom)*grad
    return p_new, v, C, score
```
To save, in loop keep `C` from previous p. Start C=conv(p). Then:
```
while time < phase_end:
    P=rfft(p,N)
    # C should be from P? Recompute to be safe:
    C=irfft(P*P,N)[:L]; C[C<0]=0
    z=beta*C - z.max()
    Z=exp(z); sumZ=Z.sum(); if sumZ==0: Z=np.zeros(L); Z[C.argmax()]=1 else Z/=sumZ
    corr=irfft(rfft(Z,N)*np.conj(P),N)[:n]
    grad=p+corr
    v=mom*v+(1-mom)*grad
    # maybe scale v by 1/(max(v)+eps)?
    # Try one or two lrs
    best_try=p; best_try_score=score
    for lr_try in (lr, lr*0.5, lr*0.25):
        q=np.clip(p - lr_try*v,0,None)
        s=q.sum()
        if s>0:
            q/=s
            # score only maybe every k? If cheap:
            s2=fft_score(q)
            if s2 < best_try_score: ...
    if best_try is not p: p=best_try; score=best_try_score
    else: maybe scale lr down
    if score < best_score: best...
```
This does up to 3 score FFTs plus gradient conv/corr. Too heavy. We can reduce: Use only one lr_try chosen adaptively, and compute score via the C that would be needed next? Actually after p_try, we can compute its conv as part of next iteration's C. But we need choose. We can accept p_try without scoring, and score it at top of next iteration. If it is bad, we can revert? Keep old p backup. Simpler: no line search; just one deterministic step with fixed lr. We can monitor actual score at beginning of each iteration. If score increases substantially for many steps, reduce lr.

Let's do:
```
for it:
  compute C,score from p at start (or store from previous end)
  if score<best save
  compute gradient from C
  v update
  p_new=clip_norm(p - lr*v)
  # optional if score of p_new not computed; will be next loop. To avoid divergence, if lr too large, p_new might go zero; check min sum.
  p=p_new
  # every 5 iters, compute score and if worse than best by >factor, reduce lr and maybe revert to best? 
```
But if we don't score, we may waste time wandering. We can score p_new by computing its C and store for next iteration: after p_new, compute P_new and C_new (one conv) and score; then use C_new as current C next loop, avoiding recomputation. But we still need gradient with C_new; that requires ZF and corr next loop using P_new. We already have P_new. So per iteration:
- We have P_current and C_current from previous step.
- Compute Z, ZF, corr using P_current.
- grad, v, p_new.
- Compute P_new and C_new (one rfft+irfft) and score_new.
- Set p=P_new? Actually p_new vector; P_new, C_current=C_new, P_current=P_new for next.
Thus per iteration: 2 rffts (P_current, P_new) + 1 ZF rfft +2 irffts (corr, C_new) = 3 forward,2 inverse. If we also compute grad conv from P_current*P_current? We can get C_new? We have P_current from previous, but need C_current. We computed C_current from P_new in previous iteration? Actually C_current is from P_current. In previous step, after p_new we computed P_new and C_new. So C_current available. We don't need irfft P_current^2. Great! Thus per iteration, after initial C, we need for current: ZF (rfft), corr (irfft); for new: P_new (rfft), C_new (irfft P_new^2). So 3 FFTs (rfft Z, rfft P_new, irfft corr, irfft C_new? that's 4 transforms: rfft Z, rfft P_new, irfft products corr and C_new). The previous P_current can be reused from previous new. So 4 transforms/iter. Good.

Algorithm:
```
P = rfft(p,N); C = irfft(P*P,N)[:L]; score=...
v=zeros
while time:
  Z from C
  ZF = rfft(Z,N)
  corr = irfft(ZF*conj(P),N)[:n]
  grad = p+corr
  v = mom*v + (1-mom)*grad
  p_new = clip_norm(p - lr*v)
  if all zero: p_new=p; break/reduce
  P_new = rfft(p_new,N)
  C_new = irfft(P_new*P_new,N)[:L]; C_new<=0 clip
  score_new=2n*C_new.max()
  # adaptive: if score_new > score*(1+0.1) and fail_count++ reduce lr; else accept
  accept = True
  if score_new > best_score and ... maybe reduce; but we can still accept to explore? To avoid blow up, if score_new > prev_score*1.2, revert and lr*=0.5.
  if accept:
     p=p_new; P=P_new; C=C_new; score=score_new
  else:
     lr*=0.5; v = v*0.5? try again
  if score<best: best=p.copy()
  # kick if stall
```
This uses the new C for next. Score_new computed exactly. Good.

Adaptive step: Start lr maybe 0.2? Need scale. Since v is gradient with momentum. We can normalize v to have L2 norm equal to something. What scale? p ~1/√n at edge, 1/n typical. Gradient ~1/n typical, edge maybe 1/n? v typical ~1/n. If lr=1, update typical 1/n. For p typical 1/n, relative large; maybe lr=0.1. But if gradient smaller due to beta? Let's use a normalized gradient: `g = grad - grad.mean()`? Since subtracting a constant shifts p but normalization removes constant? If we subtract mean, update focuses on shape, scale smaller. The projection/normalization already handles constant. Using centered gradient may improve: grad_c = grad - grad.mean(). Then all updates sum zero before normalize. Its L2 norm can be controlled: `g_scale = np.linalg.norm(grad_c)`; if g_scale>0, `v = mom*v + (1-mom)*(grad_c/g_scale)`; then lr is a dimensionless step in units of root variance. This is more stable. But does it destroy direction? It's a directional derivative scaled to unit norm. Then lr can be ~0.1. The objective scale not considered, but for L∞ max, moving shape with unit gradient norm may be okay. Alternatively use no normalization and lr=1/n? Let's test: grad_c typical values O(1/n); L2 norm sqrt(n)*(1/n)=1/√n. If normalize, direction with components O(1/n). lr=0.01 gives step O(0.01/√n) much smaller than p typical 1/n? For n=4096, 0.01/64=0.000156; p typical 0.000244, relative 0.6; good. For edge p0=0.0156, relative0.01. Could be okay.

Maybe use `step = lr * (grad_c / (np.linalg.norm(grad_c) + 1e-12))` with lr ~0.05-0.2. This is like using a unit step direction. But GD should scale with curvature; unit norm may be too aggressive in flat directions. We can tune phases: lr 0.05 coarse, 0.01 fine.

Alternatively, scale by mean p: `scale = np.mean(p)` (1/n). `v = mom*v + (1-mom)*(grad_c/scale)` then update p - lr*scale? Equivalent. Let's use centered gradient normalized by its L∞ or L2.

Let's design `make_step(p, v, lr, mom)`:
```
corr = ...
grad = p + corr
grad = grad - grad.mean()  # remove constant
gnorm = np.linalg.norm(grad)
if gnorm > 0:
    grad = grad / gnorm
v = mom*v + (1-mom)*grad
p_new = p - lr*v
clip norm
```
Momentum v is unit-ish. lr dimensionless. Try lr values 0.02-0.2.

Could also use `grad = p*grad - p*grad.mean()p`? That's softmax gradient. For simplex, the natural gradient (Fisher) is `p_i*(grad_i - avg p_j grad_j)`. This may be more stable for nonnegative. Let's consider. If we parameterize p=exp(x), objective gradient w.r.t x is p⊙(grad - <p,grad>). The Newton/FG step on x with normalized gradient: direction d = (grad - mean_p) maybe; update x += -eta*d; p=softmax(x). Equivalent p_new ∝ p exp(-eta*(grad - mean_p)). Multiplicative update. This may preserve positivity and handle scale. Let's use this! It's a natural gradient / mirror descent on simplex.

Log-sumexp objective L(p). Gradient on p space grad. The natural gradient for p with simplex and entropy regularization is `p * (grad - ∑p grad)`. For log-sumexp, `grad_i = p_i + corr_i`. Then natural step direction = p_i*(grad_i - mean_p_grad). We can normalize and use exponential update:
```
mu = (p*grad).sum()
d = grad - mu
# d can be positive/negative, and ∑ p_i d_i=0.
# Normalize d by its p-weighted norm? 
dnorm = sqrt(∑ p_i d_i^2)
if dnorm>0: d = d/dnorm
x_new = log(p) - lr*d? But p may have zeros; instead multiplicative:
p_new = p * exp(-lr*d) / sum
```
Since ∑ p_i d_i=0, the sum of p exp(-lr*d) is close to1; normalization anyway. This automatically keeps nonnegative, no clipping needed! Very stable. The step size lr controls relative changes; if d normalized (p-weighted), exp(-lr*d) changes each p by factor exp(±lr). For lr=0.1, 10% relative changes. That's nice.

Let's derive: We want minimize L. Mirror descent with Bregman KL on simplex: p_new = argmin L(p_old) - <grad, p-p_old> + (1/η) KL(p||p_old). Solution p_new ∝ p_old * exp(-η grad). But since sum p new=1, constant. Adding constant to grad doesn't matter. Use grad_centered = grad - (∑p grad)/∑p (sum1) to improve conditioning. Then p_new = p * exp(-η * grad_centered) / sum. This is exactly multiplicative. It can be more effective than additive for nonnegative variables. The score can improve. Let's adopt this as primary optimizer! It avoids simplex projection and zeros. It also has natural scale: deviation in p relative.

Gradient `grad = p + corr`. But for natural gradient of L w.r.t x (log p) is p⊙grad (if L as function of p). The mirror update uses p⊙exp(-η grad). We can just use `p * exp(-η*grad)`; constant not matter. But grad values O(1/n), η would need O(n). We can normalize grad to have typical scale 1. Use centered and p-weighted normalized d. Let `d = grad - mu`, `dnorm = sqrt(np.sum(p*d*d))`, if >0 d/=dnorm. Then η is relative step (0.05-0.5). This should work.

Update:
```
p_new = p * np.exp(-lr * d)
s=p_new.sum(); if s<=0 or not finite: fallback clip additive
p_new /= s
```
Since ∑ p_i * d_i =0, first-order sum unchanged; normalization accounts second-order. This is stable.

Momentum: We can maintain `v` in d space: `v = mom*v + (1-mom)*d`; use p*exp(-lr*v). Since ∑p v may not be zero due to p changes; center v with p: `v = v - (p*v).sum()` before update. Good.

This natural gradient is essentially multiplicative weights on the log-sumexp loss. It should be good for nonnegative sequences. Let's use it.

We can also do line search over lr values [0.1,0.05,0.2] by computing score of p_new (which we need anyway). Since update is cheap, we can try 2-3 lr per iteration. But each p_new score requires FFT; maybe okay if we compute P_new and C_new. We can choose best among lr and maybe old. To keep cost modest, do one lr with adaptive; occasionally line search.

- If no improvement for 200 iterations, add a random multiplicative perturbation: p *= exp(rng.normal(0, noise_scale, n)); normalize; reset v; noise_scale maybe 0.05-0.2. This scans nearby functions.
- Periodically, if score stuck, increase beta to focus on max.

Let's detail candidate generation:
```
def generate_candidates(n, rng, max_candidates=None):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    cands=[]
    def add(p): p=np.asanyarray; if finite and sum>0: p=np.maximum(p,0); p=p/p.sum(); s=_score_fft(p); cands.append((s,p)); 
    base = x**-0.5; add(base)
    add(base[::-1])
    alphas = [0.2,0.3,0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55]
    for a in alphas: add(x**(-a))
    # exponential tapers
    for lam in [0.0,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0,8.0]: add(base*np.exp(-lam*x))
    for lam in [0.0,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0]: add(base*np.exp(-lam*(1-x))) # shifts? maybe
    # power bumps
    for a in [0.25,0.5,1,2,4]:
      for b in [0.25,0.5,1,2,4]:
         h=x**a*(1-x)**b
         if h.max()>0:
            hn=h/h.sum()
            for w in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5]:
               add((1-w)*base/w? Actually normalize((1-w)*base + w*hn))
               # notch
               ratio=h/h.max() if h.max()>0 else 0
               p=base*(1-w*ratio)
               if p.min()>=-1e-9: add(p)
    # shifted inverse differences
    for M in [n//8,n//4,n//2,3*n//4]:
       v=1/np.sqrt(np.arange(1,n+1))
       vs=np.zeros(n); if M<n: vs[:n-M]=v[M:] ? v_{i+M}=1/sqrt(i+M+1)
       for q in [0.2,0.5,0.8]: add(v - q*vs)
    # telescoping sqrt
    r=np.sqrt(np.arange(1,n+1))-np.sqrt(np.arange(n)); add(r)
    # randoms
    for _ in range(20): add(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.5))
    sort by score; return top unique.
```

Natural optimize implementation detail with phases and kicks:
```
def optimize_sequence(p, end_time, target_score=1.49, verbose=True):
    p=_normalize(p)
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2(3*n-1)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)[:L]; C=np.maximum(C,0)
    score=2.0*n*C.max()
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score
    v=np.zeros(n)
    eta=0.15; mom=0.75
    # phases list (target_beta, base_eta, momentum, duration_fraction)
    phases=[(20,0.20,0.70,0.2),(50,0.15,0.8,0.25),(100,0.10,0.85,0.25),(200,0.06,0.9,0.2),(500,0.03,0.95,0.1)]
    total_time=end_time-time.monotonic()
    stall=0
    for target, base_eta, mom, frac in phases:
       phase_end=min(end_time, time.monotonic()+frac*total_time)
       while time.monotonic()<phase_end and time.monotonic()<end_time:
          cmax=C.max()
          if cmax<=0: break
          beta=target/cmax
          z=beta*C; z-=z.max(); Z=np.exp(z); sZ=Z.sum()
          if not finite or sZ==0: Z=np.zeros(L); Z[int(C.argmax())]=1
          else: Z/=sZ
          ZF=np.fft.rfft(Z,N)
          corr=np.fft.irfft(ZF*np.conj(P),N)[:n]
          corr=np.maximum(corr,0?) Corr of nonneg should nonneg but roundoff; clip tiny negative 0.
          grad=p+corr
          mu=float(np.dot(p,grad))
          d=grad-mu
          dmax=np.max(np.abs(d))
          if dmax < 1e-15:
             break
          d=d/dmax
          v=mom*v+(1-mom)*d
          # recenter and clip
          v=v - float(np.dot(p,v))
          vmax=np.max(np.abs(v))
          if vmax>5: v=v*(5/vmax)
          p_new=p*np.exp(-eta*v)
          p_new=np.maximum(p_new,0)
          s=p_new.sum()
          if s<=0 or not finite: p_new=p.copy()
          else: p_new/=s
          # compute P_new,C_new
          P_n=np.fft.rfft(p_new,N)
          C_n=np.fft.irfft(P_n*P_n,N)[:L]
          C_n=np.maximum(C_n,0)
          score_n=2*n*C_n.max()
          if not finite: score_n=np.inf
          if score_n < score and score_n < best_score*1.5:
             # accept
             p=p_new; P=P_n; C=C_n; score=score_n
             if score<best_score:
                best=p.copy(); best_score=score; stall=0
                # eta = min(eta*1.05, base_eta)
             else: stall+=1
          else:
             stall+=1
             if score_n > score*1.5:
                eta*=0.5
             else:
                # still accept? If score_n only slightly worse, accept to escape? Maybe accept but not if worse than best too much.
                accepts = rng.random() < 0.3 if score_n < best_score*1.1 else False
                if accepts:
                   p=p_new; P=P_n; C=C_n; score=score_n
             if stall > 300:
                # random kick
                noise=np.log(max(eta,0.02)*rng.normal(0,1,n)? )
```
Need robust not too complex. Maybe always accept p_new if it doesn't diverge; natural gradient with small eta likely locally decreases smoothed objective even if exact score may oscillate. Detailed functions:
```
import numpy as np, time

def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << int(m-1).bit_length()
```
Actually for int m, `int((m-1).bit_length())` returns ceil log2. Good.

`_normalize(p)`:
```
def _normalize(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim!=1 or p.size==0: return p
    if not np.isfinite(p).all(): p=np.where(np.isfinite(p),p,0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.ones(p.size)/p.size
    return p/s
```

`_score_fft(p)`:
```
def _score_fft(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    if p.size==0: return np.inf
    p=np.maximum(p,0)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(s): return np.inf
    p=p/s
    n=p.size; L=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2(L)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)[:L]
    C[C<0]=0
    return float(2.0*n*C.max())
```
If n large, direct P*P complex. Good.

`_recompute_pC(p,N)`:
```
def _fft_pC(p,N):
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    L=2*len(p)-1
    C=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)[:L]
    C[C<0]=0.0
    return P,C
```
No closures.

def _kick(p, rng, scale):

    p2 = p * np.exp(scale * rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n))
    if rng.random() < 0.3:
       th = 0.1 + 0.3*rng.random()
       p2 = (1.0-th)*p2 + th*p2[::-1]

def _try_add(cands, p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    if p.size==0 or not np.isfinite(p).all(): return
    p=_normalize(p)

`_generate_candidates(n, rng, max_candidates)` top-level:
```
def _generate_candidates(n, rng):
    cands=[]
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
    xm1=1.0-x
    base=x**(-0.5)
    _try_add(cands, base)
    _try_add(cands, base[::-1])
    alphas=[0.2,0.3,0.35,0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]
    for a in alphas: _try_add(cands, x**(-a))
    for lam in [0.0,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0,8.0]:
       _try_add(cands, base*np.exp(-lam*x))
       _try_add(cands, base[::-1]*np.exp(-lam*x))
       _try_add(cands, base*np.exp(-lam*xm1))
    boosts=[(0.25,0.25), ...]
    for a,b in ...:
       h=x**a * xm1**b
       if h.max()<=0: continue
       hn=h/h.sum()
       for w in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5]:
          _try_add(cands, (1.0-w)*base + w*hn)
          ratio=h/h.max()
          _try_add(cands, base*(1.0 - w*ratio))
          _try_add(cands, base/(1.0 + w*ratio))  # smooth notch
    # shifts
    v=1.0/np.sqrt(np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64))
    for M in [max(1,n//8), n//4, n//2, 3*n//4]:
       vs=np.zeros(n)
       if M < n:
          vs[:n-M] = v[M:]  # v_{i+M+1}? v index M corresponds 1/sqrt(M+1)
       for q in [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]:
          _try_add(cands, v - q*vs)
          _try_add(cands, v + q*vs)
          # also reversed mix
    # telescoping
    r=np.sqrt(np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float))-np.sqrt(np.arange(n,dtype=float))
    _try_add(cands, r)
    # random Dirichlet
    for alpha in [0.05,0.1,0.5,2.0]:
       for _ in range(5): _try_add(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    return cands
```
Need avoid too many candidates? Maybe 500; okay. But scoring each with FFT at n=4096 maybe 500*~0.1ms? Actually _score_fft uses N=8192? For scoring only N=next_pow2(2n-1)=8192, faster. 500*2 transforms ~1000 transforms, maybe 0.5s. Fine.

But `_try_add` uses `_score_fft` which normalizes; for candidates like base sum large, okay. It returns copy p; memory ~500*4096*8=16MB, okay. We can restrict: after appending, if len>2000? Not needed. But bump loops 5x5x6*3=450 + exps 21 + random60 + shifts ~40 =570.

def _is_distinct(p, starts, tol=1e-2):
    for s,q in starts:
       if np.max(np.abs(p-q)) < tol: return False
    return True

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start + max(10.0, float(budget_s)-20.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # choose n: 4096; if parent length available and nicer maybe n_parent but we can still use 4096.
    n=4096
    cands=_generate_candidates(n,rng)
    # parent add
    try: ...
    cands.sort(key=_score_key)
    print top
    starts=[]
    for s,p in cands[:50]:
       if _is_distinct(p,starts,1e-3): starts.append((s,p)); if len(starts)>=6: break
    global_best_p = starts[0][1].copy() if starts else np.ones(n)/n
    global_best_score = starts[0][0] if starts else 2.0
    # time allocation
    remaining=deadline-time.monotonic()
    weights=[0.4,0.2,0.15,0.1,0.075,0.075]
    for i in range(len(starts)):
       share = remaining*weights[i] if i < len(weights) else remaining*0.05/...
       if share < 20: share = min(20, remaining*0.05)
       end = time.monotonic() + share
       # If current time greater than deadline break
       p_best, sc_best = _optimize_natural(starts[i][1], end, rng, verbose=len(starts)<=3)
       if sc_best < global_best_score:
           global_best_score=sc_best; global_best_p=p_best.copy()
           print(...)
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
    # Final local search from global best with remaining time using natural optimize with high target?
    if time.monotonic() < deadline-5:
       # run another optimization from global best for remaining/2
       p2,sc2 = _optimize_natural(global_best_p, deadline-2, rng, verbose=False)
       if sc2 < global_best_score: update
    # Ensure final nonnegative list length maybe if any value >1000? p normalized sum 1, values <=1.
    final = np.clip(global_best_p,0,1000)
    # optional evaluate_sequence to verify? If final length manageable (<10000) and time? We can call:
    try:
       ev=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final])
       print(ev)
       # If ev is much worse than global_best_score plus 0.02, maybe use parent? Not necessary.
    except: pass
    return [float(x) for x in final]
```

Let's refine `_optimize_natural` to be robust and not too long.

Pseudo code full:
```
def _optimize_natural(p0, end_time, rng, verbose=True):
    p=_normalize(p0)
    n=p.size
    if n<8: p=np.ones(64)/64; n=64
    L=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2(3*n-1)
    P,C=_fft_pC(p,N)
    score=float(2.0*n*C.max())
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score
    if verbose: print(...)
    if end_time - time.monotonic() <=2: return best,best_score
    total=max(1e-6, end_time-time.monotonic())
    phases=[(20,0.25,0.65,0.12),(50,0.2,0.75,0.23),(100,0.12,0.85,0.30),(250,0.07,0.9,0.22),(500,0.04,0.95,0.13)]
    for phase in phases:
       target, base_eta, mom, frac = phase
       phase_end = time.monotonic() + frac*total
       if phase_end > end_time: phase_end=end_time
       eta=base_eta
       v=np.zeros(n)
       stall=0
       bad=0
       it=0
       while time.monotonic() < phase_end:
          it+=1
          cmax=float(C.max())
          if cmax <= 1e-300: break
          beta=target/cmax
          z=beta*(C-cmax)
          # z max 0, min -target
          Z=np.exp(z)
          sz=float(Z.sum())
          if not np.isfinite(sz) or sz <= 0:
             Z=np.zeros(L); Z[int(np.argmax(C))]=1.0
          else:
             Z=Z/sz
          ZF=np.fft.rfft(Z,N)
          corr=np.fft.irfft(ZF*np.conj(P),N)[:n]
          corr=np.maximum(corr,0.0)
          grad=p+corr
          mu=float(np.dot(p,grad))
          d=grad-mu
          dmax=float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
          if dmax <= 1e-15:
             break
          d=d/dmax
          v=mom*v+(1.0-mom)*d
          v=v - float(np.dot(p,v))
          vmax=float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
          if vmax > 5.0:
             v=v*(5.0/vmax)
          p_new=p*np.exp(-eta*v)
          p_new=np.maximum(p_new,0.0)
          s=float(p_new.sum())
          if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0:
             p_new=p.copy()
             s=1.0
          else:
             p_new=p_new/s
          P_n,C_n=_fft_pC(p_new,N)
          score_n=float(2.0*n*C_n.max())
          if not np.isfinite(score_n):
             score_n=np.inf
          accept=False
          if score_n <= score * 1.15 or score_n < best_score * 1.05 or rng.random() < 0.25:
              accept=True
          if score_n > max(score*2.0, best_score*1.5):
              accept=False
              eta*=0.5
              stall+=5
          if accept:
              p=p_new; P=P_n; C=C_n; score=score_n
              if score < best_score:
                  best=p.copy(); best_score=score; stall=0
                  eta=min(eta*1.03, base_eta*1.2)
              else:
                  stall+=1
          else:
              stall+=1
              if eta > base_eta*0.1:
                  eta*=0.7
              else:
                  # kick
                  p=_kick(p,rng,0.1+0.3*rng.random())
                  P,C=_fft_pC(p,N)
                  score=float(2.0*n*C.max())
                  v=np.zeros(n); eta=base_eta; stall=0
          if score > best_score * 2.0:
              # reset to best sometimes
              if rng.random() < 0.2:
                  p=best.copy(); P,C=_fft_pC(p,N); score=best_score; v=np.zeros(n); eta=base_eta
          if stall > 400:
              p=_kick(p,rng,0.1+0.3*rng.random())
              P,C=_fft_pC(p,N); score=float(2.0*n*C.max())
              v=np.zeros(n); eta=base_eta; stall=0
          if verbose and it % 2000 ==0:
              print(...)
       # end phase: if current too bad vs best, reset to best for next phase
       if score > best_score * 1.5:
           p=best.copy(); P,C=_fft_pC(p,N); score=best_score
    # maybe final quick polish from best using remaining
    if time.monotonic() + 5 < end_time:
       # set to best and one high beta phase for remaining
       p=best.copy(); P,C=_fft_pC(p,N); score=best_score
       target=1000.0; base_eta=0.02; mom=0.95
       eta=base_eta; v=np.zeros(n); stall=0
       while time.monotonic() + 2 < end_time:
          ... same small loop
    if verbose: print
    return best,best_score
```

    p = _normalize(p0)

```python
import numpy as np
import time


def _next_pow2(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(p):
    """Clip to nonnegative and normalize to sum one."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1 or p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(p.size)
    return p / s


def _score_fft(p):
    """Evaluate 2*n*max(conv(p,p))/sum(p)**2 using FFT."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    p = p / s
    n = p.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(L)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(P * P, N)[:L]
    C[C < 0.0] = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * float(C.max()))


def _fft_pC(p, N):
    """Return FFT of p and normalized convolution p*p."""
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    L = 2 * p.size - 1
    C = np.fft.irfft(P * P, N)[:L]
    C[C < 0.0] = 0.0
    return P, C


def _score_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _is_distinct(p, starts, tol=1e-3):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    for _, q in starts:
        q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.size != q.size:
            continue
        if np.max(np.abs(p - q)) < tol:
            return False
    return True


def _try_add(cands, p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(p).all():
        return
    p = _normalize(p)
    if np.min(p) < 0.0:
        return
    s = _score_fft(p)
    if np.isfinite(s):
        cands.append((float(s), p.copy()))


def _generate_candidates(n, rng):
    """Build a broad set of nonnegative initial shapes."""
    cands = []
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    y = 1.0 - x

    base = x ** (-0.5)
    _try_add(cands, base)
    _try_add(cands, base[::-1])

    for a in (0.20, 0.30, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60):
        _try_add(cands, x ** (-a))

    # Logarithmic tilts. These redistribute mass left-to-right or right-to-left.
    for lam in (0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        _try_add(cands, base * np.exp(-lam * x))
        _try_add(cands, base * np.exp(lam * x))
        _try_add(cands, base * np.exp(-lam * y))
        _try_add(cands, base * np.exp(lam * y))
        _try_add(cands, base[::-1] * np.exp(-lam * x))
        _try_add(cands, base[::-1] * np.exp(-lam * y))

    # Linear tilts / rational tapers.
    for a in (-0.5, -0.2, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0):
        p1 = base * (1.0 + a * x)
        p2 = base * (1.0 + a * y)
        _try_add(cands, p1)
        _try_add(cands, p2)
        _try_add(cands, base / (1.0 + a * x))
        _try_add(cands, base / (1.0 + a * y))

    # Smooth bumps / notches.
    boosts = (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0)
    weights = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.50)
    for a in boosts:
        xa = x ** a
        for b in boosts:
            h = xa * (y ** b)
            hmax = float(h.max())
            if hmax <= 0.0:
                continue
            hn = h / float(h.sum())
            ratio = h / hmax
            for w in weights:
                _try_add(cands, (1.0 - w) * base + w * hn)
                _try_add(cands, base * (1.0 - w * ratio))
                _try_add(cands, base / (1.0 + w * ratio))

    # Shifted inverse-sqrt differences.
    v = 1.0 / np.sqrt(np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64))
    shifts = (max(1, n // 8), max(1, n // 4), max(1, n // 2), max(1, 3 * n // 4))
    for M in shifts:
        if M >= n:
            continue
        vs = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        vs[:n - M] = v[M:]
        for q in (0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0):
            _try_add(cands, v - q * vs)
            _try_add(cands, v + q * vs)
            _try_add(cands, v[::-1] - q * vs[::-1])
            _try_add(cands, v[::-1] + q * vs[::-1])

    # Telescoping square-root increment.
    r = np.sqrt(np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)) - np.sqrt(np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64))
    _try_add(cands, r)
    _try_add(cands, r[::-1])

    # A few random Dirichlet perturbations for diversity.
    for alpha in (0.20, 0.50, 1.00, 2.00):
        for _ in range(4):
            _try_add(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha, dtype=np.float64)))

    return cands


def _kick(p, rng, scale):
    """Multiplicative random perturbation, optionally mixed with reverse."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    p2 = p * np.exp(scale * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n))
    if rng.random() < 0.35:
        th = 0.05 + 0.30 * rng.random()
        p2 = (1.0 - th) * p2 + th * p2[::-1]
    return _normalize(p2)


def _optimize_natural(p0, end_time, rng, verbose=True):
    """
    Natural-gradient / multiplicative projected search on the probability simplex.

    The smooth objective is log-sum-exp(beta * conv(p,p)).  The gradient is
    dL/dp = p + correlate(softmax(beta*C), p).  Wephercent it with the current
    p-moment and update multiplicatively: p <- exp(-eta*d) p, renormalized.
    """
    p = _normalize(p0)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n < 16:
        p = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
        n = 256

    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(3 * n - 1)
    P, C = _fft_pC(p, N)
    score = float(2.0 * n * float(C.max()))

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = score

    start_time = time.monotonic()
    if verbose:
        print(f"optimize start n={n} initial={score:.6f}", flush=True)
    if end_time - start_time < 2.0:
        return best, best_score

    total = max(1e-6, end_time - start_time)

    # (target beta * Cmax, base multiplicative step, momentum, fraction of time)
    phase_specs = [
        (20.0, 0.25, 0.65, 0.10),
        (50.0, 0.18, 0.75, 0.20),
        (120.0, 0.11, 0.85, 0.25),
        (300.0, 0.06, 0.90, 0.20),
        (800.0, 0.03, 0.95, 0.15),
        (1600.0, 0.016, 0.95, 0.10),
    ]

    for target_beta, base_eta, mom, frac in phase_specs:
        phase_end = start_time + frac * total
        if phase_end > end_time:
            phase_end = end_time

        eta = float(base_eta)
        v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        stall = 0
        it = 0
        last_out = time.monotonic()

        while time.monotonic() < phase_end:
            it += 1
            cmax = float(C.max())
            if cmax <= 1e-300:
                break

            beta = target_beta / cmax
            z = beta * (C - cmax)
            Z = np.exp(z)
            zsum = float(Z.sum())
            if (not np.isfinite(zsum)) or zsum <= 0.0:
                Z = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
                Z[int(np.argmax(C))] = 1.0
            else:
                Z = Z / zsum

            ZF = np.fft.rfft(Z, N)
            corr = np.fft.irfft(ZF * np.conj(P), N)[:n]
            corr = np.maximum(corr, 0.0)

            grad = p + corr
            mu = float(np.dot(p, grad))
            d = grad - mu
            dmax = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
            if dmax <= 1e-15:
                break

            d = d / dmax
            v = mom * v + (1.0 - mom) * d
            v = v - float(np.dot(p, v))

            vmax = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
            if vmax > 5.0:
                v = v * (5.0 / vmax)

            p_new = p * np.exp(-eta * v)
            p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 0.0)
            s = float(p_new.sum())
            if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
                p_new = p.copy()
                s = 1.0
            else:
                p_new = p_new / s

            P_new, C_new = _fft_pC(p_new, N)
            score_new = float(2.0 * n * float(C_new.max()))
            if not np.isfinite(score_new):
                score_new = np.inf

            accept = False
            if np.isfinite(score_new):
                if score_new > max(2.0 * score, 1.5 * best_score):
                    accept = False
                elif score_new <= 1.15 * score:
                    accept = True
                elif score_new < 1.05 * best_score:
                    accept = True
                elif rng.random() < 0.25:
                    accept = True

            if accept:
                p = p_new
                P = P_new
                C = C_new
                score = score_new
                if score < best_score:
                    best = p.copy()
                    best_score = score
                    stall = 0
                    eta = min(eta * 1.03, 1.2 * base_eta)
                else:
                    stall += 1
            else:
                stall += 1
                if eta > 0.1 * base_eta:
                    eta *= 0.7
                else:
                    p = _kick(p, rng, 0.10 + 0.25 * rng.random())
                    P, C = _fft_pC(p, N)
                    score = float(2.0 * n * float(C.max()))
                    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    eta = float(base_eta)
                    stall = 0

            if not np.isfinite(score) or score > 2.0 * best_score:
                if rng.random() < 0.25:
                    p = best.copy()
                    P, C = _fft_pC(p, N)
                    score = best_score
                    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    eta = float(base_eta)
                    stall = 0

            if stall > 350:
                p = _kick(p, rng, 0.10 + 0.30 * rng.random())
                P, C = _fft_pC(p, N)
                score = float(2.0 * n * float(C.max()))
                v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                eta = float(base_eta)
                stall = 0

            if verbose and time.monotonic() - last_out > 20.0:
                print(
                    f"phase beta={target_beta:.0f} t={time.monotonic()-start_time:.1f}s "
                    f"local={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f}",
                    flush=True
                )
                last_out = time.monotonic()

        if score > 1.5 * best_score:
            p = best.copy()
            P, C = _fft_pC(p, N)
            score = best_score

    if verbose:
        print(f"optimize done n={p.size} best={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)
    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for low-maximum autocorrelation nonnegative sequences.

    Strategy:
      - Generate many structured one-sided inverse-square-root-like candidates
        (tilts, bumps, notches, shifted inverse differences, random Dirichlet).
      - Score them exactly by the FFT version of the evaluation objective.
      - Run natural multiplicative projected gradient descent on the probability
        simplex from the best diverse starts.
      - Keep the globally best vector and return it.
    """
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0)
    deadline = start_time + budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if budget < 30.0:
        n = 1024
        x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
        p = _normalize(x ** (-0.5))
        print("short budget, returning inverse-sqrt candidate", flush=True)
        return [float(v) for v in p]

    n = 4096
    cands = _generate_candidates(n, rng)

    # Include the previously supplied construction if it is usable.
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except NameError:
        parent = None
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        parent = parent.ravel()
        if 256 <= parent.size <= 10000 and np.all(np.isfinite(parent)):
            par = _normalize(parent)
            par_score = _score_fft(par)
            if np.isfinite(par_score):
                cands.append((float(par_score), par.copy()))
                print(f"parent candidate n={par.size} score={par_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    cands.sort(key=_score_key)

    if not cands:
        x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
        p = _normalize(x ** (-0.5))
        s = _score_fft(p)
        cands = [(float(s), p)]

    starts = []
    for s, p in cands[:80]:
        if _is_distinct(p, starts, tol=5e-3):
            starts.append((float(s), np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)))
            if len(starts) >= 6:
                break

    if not starts:
        x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
        p = _normalize(x ** (-0.5))
        s = _score_fft(p)
        starts = [(float(s), p)]

    for i, (s, p) in enumerate(starts[:6]):
        print(f"start {i}: n={p.size} fft_score={s:.6f}", flush=True)

    global_best_p = np.asarray(starts[0][1], dtype=np.float64).copy()
    global_best_score = float(starts[0][0])

    # Allocate time to the most promising starts.
    total_alloc = max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    shares = (0.45, 0.25, 0.15, 0.10, 0.05, 0.05)

    for i, (s, p) in enumerate(starts[:6]):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        share = total_alloc * shares[i]
        if share < 10.0:
            share = min(20.0, max(5.0, 0.05 * total_alloc))
        end = time.monotonic() + share
        if end > deadline:
            end = deadline
        verbose = (i < 2)
        p_best, s_best = _optimize_natural(p, end, rng, verbose=verbose)
        if s_best < global_best_score:
            global_best_score = float(s_best)
            global_best_p = np.asarray(p_best, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"new global best after start {i}: {global_best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Use any remaining time to polish the current global best.
    if time.monotonic() + 10.0 < deadline:
        p_polish, s_polish = _optimize_natural(
            global_best_p,
            deadline - 5.0,
            rng,
            verbose=False
        )
        if s_polish < global_best_score:
            global_best_score = float(s_polish)
            global_best_p = np.asarray(p_polish, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"polish improved global best: {global_best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    final = np.asarray(global_best_p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    final = np.where(np.isfinite(final), final, 0.0)
    final = np.maximum(final, 0.0)
    final = np.minimum(final, 1000.0)
    s = float(final.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        final = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
    else:
        # Return a scale-invariant probability vector; the evaluator normalizes.
        final = final / s

    # Optional exact check, if time permits. The evaluator uses direct convolve;
    # keep final length moderate.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0 and final.size <= 6000:
        try:
            exact = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in final])
            print(f"exact evaluator score: {exact}", flush=True)
            if not np.isfinite(exact):
                final = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
        except Exception:
            pass

    return [float(v) for v in final]
```
